### ` PD Summer Schedule.pdf`

- **tags:** `summer-pd-2026`, `cte-professional-development`, `curriculum-integration`, `technology-training`, `compliance-procedures`, `work-based-learning`, `platform-training`, `showcase-2026`, `scheduling`, `pd-kickoff`, `keynote-speaker`, `labor-market-assessment`, `wbl-what-does-this-look-like`, `student-success`, `ai-lessons`, `google-gemini-notebooklm`, `adobe-training`, `optimal-ed-vr`, `certiport`, `everfi`
- **entities:** `Jeremy Spence`, `Daphne Kent`, `EIF`, `Pathful`, `Optimal Ed`, `Google`, `Adobe`, `Certiport`, `Everfi`, `iCEV`, `Eduthings`, `Mad Learn`
- **artifacts:** `schedule`, `training-calendar`, `session-description`, `pd-program`, `compliance-guide`, `platform-overview`
- **summary:** This document is the 2026 Summer Professional Development schedule for Dallas ISD Career & Technical Education staff, spanning multiple days in June with sessions organized by theme (College & Career Readiness, Technology & Innovation, Operations & Compliance, and Instructional Resources). It serves as a centralized roadmap for educator training on platform integration (Google Gemini, Adobe, iCEV), compliance (lab safety, procurement, travel), and work-based learning strategies. The schedule includes keynote speakers, vendor trainings, and breakout sessions across multiple rooms, concluding with a feedback collection mechanism. It matters because it aligns staff development with CTE program goals, ensures regulatory compliance, and introduces new tools to elevate student outcomes.

### `2024-2025-clna-guidebook.pdf`

- **tags:** `perkins-v-clna`, `2024-2025-cte-guidebook`, `comprehensive-local-needs-assessment`, `student-performance-evaluation`, `labor-market-alignment`, `programs-of-study-quality`, `cte-educator-recruitment-retention`, `equity-and-access-improvement`, `stakeholder-engagement-advisory-committee`, `teal-cter-data-system`, `perkins-v-required-uses-of-funds`, `texas-cte-compliance`
- **entities:** `Texas Education Agency (TEA)`, `Career and Technical Education Reports (CTER) application`, `Texas Education Agency Login (TEAL) system`, `Perkins V`, `Strengthening Career and Technical Education for the 21st Century Act`
- **artifacts:** `guidebook`, `compliance-document`, `data-collection-manual`, `planning-tool`, `stakeholder-resource`
- **summary:** This guidebook provides the structured framework and step-by-step process for Texas local education agencies to conduct the Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) under Perkins V during the 2024-2025 school year. It walks leaders through stakeholder engagement, disaggregated data review via the TEAL/CTER system, evaluation of student performance and labor market alignment, assessment of programs of study size and quality, equity and access reviews for special populations, and the synthesis of findings to inform local application development and Perkins spending decisions. The document serves as the official reference for meeting the federal mandate of a CLNA every two years.

### `2026-27 Perkins Application — Filled Tentative (PS3350).pdf`

- **tags:** `perkins-application-2026-27`, `perkins-v-local-application`, `accountability-dashboard`, `cte-performance-indicators`, `llp-targets-2026-27`, `ps3350-form`, `clna-draft-integration`, `performance-narrative`, `stakeholder-engagement`, `gap-analysis`, `improvement-strategies`, `2026-27-perkins-plan`

- **summary:** This is the 2026-27 Perkins V Local Application draft for Dallas ISD, structured around the PS3350 accountability framework. It captures current performance across nine CTE indicators—graduation rates, assessment proficiency in reading/math/science, postsecondary placement, non-traditional enrollment, credential completion, and completer rates—comparing 2023-24 actuals to state targets and LLP (Local Leadership Plan) goals for 2026-27 and 2027-28. The document includes narrative explanations for each indicator where Dallas ISD fell short of state targets, detailing root causes (notably SWD and EL gaps, systemic math/science challenges, and the impact of the 58% EB population) and specific improvement strategies such as early-warning systems, co-teaching models, industry sponsorships, and expanded college & career advising. It also outlines the continuous improvement cycle involving CLNA findings, stakeholder engagement (Industry Leadership Council, NAF boards, Dallas College), and program evaluation, with next steps focused on populating TEAL fields and confirming LLP values before submission.

- **summary:** This guidebook provides the structured process and requirements for completing the Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) under Texas Perkins V for the 2026-2027 school year. It walks local CTE leaders through seven core sections—from stakeholder engagement and student performance analysis to labor market alignment and equity improvements—ensuring that findings directly inform the local Perkins application and spending decisions. The document emphasizes the use of disaggregated data from the CTER/TEAL systems and requires advisory committees representing students, parents, industry, and special populations to drive data-driven program improvements.

The application details how grant funds will support teacher professional development, equipment purchases (including computers), and expanded program capacity, while also describing career exploration pathways for middle school students through mobile labs, showcases, and four-year planning. ### `CLNA DALLAS ISD Working Doc.pdf`

- **tags:** `clna-document`, `perkins-v-compliance`, `student-performance-analysis`, `labor-market-alignment`, `programs-of-study`, `cte-educator-recruitment`, `special-populations-gap-analysis`, `gender-race-ethnicity-performance`, `dallas-isd-cte`, `federal-accountability-indicators`

- **summary:** This CLNA working document from Dallas ISD evaluates CTE program performance, labor market alignment, and educator quality to meet Perkins V requirements. It identifies significant gaps in student performance for special populations, including students with disabilities, English learners, and economically disadvantaged students, across multiple accountability indicators. The analysis reveals enrollment gaps between high-demand career clusters (Health Science, Architecture and Construction) and actual district offerings, while highlighting strengths in Business/Marketing/Finance and STEM alignment. The document guides strategies for improving recruitment, retention, and work-based learning access, particularly for underserved student groups, and informs the local grant application and continuous improvement cycle.

### `CPAL_DISD_MOU_SowingSeeds (3).pdf`

- **tags:** `mou-2026`, `farm-to-school`, `workforce-development`, `curriculum-cte`, `partnership-agreement`, `intellectual-property`, `confidentiality`, `data-sharing`, `liability-coverage`, `exhibit-a`, `sowing-the-seeds`, `roosevelt-high-school`, `child-poverty-action-lab`, `oak-cliff-veggie-project`, `greenhouse-education`, `culinary-cte-pathway`, `local-procurement`, `cnp-integration`, `food-desert`, `two-year-pilot`
- **entities:** `Dallas Independent School District`, `Child Poverty Action Lab`, `Roosevelt High School`, `The Oak Cliff Veggie Project`, `Child Nutrition Program`, `Exhibit A`
- **artifacts:** `mou`, `governance-doc`, `exhibit`, `project-charter`, `policy-framework`
- **summary:** This May 2026 Memorandum of Understanding between Dallas ISD and Child Poverty Action Lab governs a two-year farm-to-school workforce development pilot at Roosevelt High School, activating existing greenhouses and the Culinary CTE pathway to deliver hands-on agricultural education, culinary instruction, and entrepreneurship using locally sourced produce. The agreement covers intellectual property joint ownership, confidentiality, data-sharing protocols requiring a separate DUA, indemnification, and $1M/$2M liability coverage. It aims to build a sustainable agricultural education pathway, integrate local foods into school meals, increase student contact hours, expand market opportunities for local producers, and create a replicable model for DISD, serving Oak Cliff—a designated food desert where 74.7% of students qualify for free or reduced-price meals.

It defines the structure, goals, and procedures for establishing and maintaining CTSO chapters, covering advisor responsibilities, membership, fundraising, competition eligibility, field trips, travel protocols, student behavior expectations, and stipend requirements. The document aligns CTSO activities with CTE curriculum mandates and provides standardized processes for chapter governance, finance, and safe student travel, ensuring district-wide compliance and student success.

- **summary:** This handbook defines the Campus Lead Teacher role within Dallas ISD Career and Technical Education, detailing instructional leadership, district PLC facilitation, internship coordination, and industry-based certification administration. It establishes a $2,500 annual stipend tied to measurable benchmarks—including a minimum internship goal based on 5% of CTE juniors or 10 students, whichever is greater—and a weighted evaluation rubric. The document ensures consistency across campuses, scales accountability by school size, and permits an additional Lead Teacher for campuses with more than five Programs of Study.

- **entities:** `Dallas ISD Career and Technical Education`, `Special Revenue Department – Perkins Grant Administration`, `Title I Crate`, `EDGAR procurement requirements`, `Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA)`, `Local Application`, `Perkins Local Application`, `Budget Workbook`, `Asset Management`, `Procurement`, `Warehouse`, `Conference documentation`
- **artifacts:** `standard-operating-procedure-manual`, `policy-document`, `checklist`, `workflow-chart`, `budget-dashboard-template`, `approval-matrix`, `purchasing-calendar`, `grant-amendment-form`
- **summary:** This document is the Dallas ISD CTE Standard Operating Procedures manual for Perkins V grant management, serving as the district's official operations handbook for career and technical education funding. It establishes standardized processes for budgeting, purchasing, professional development, inventory, compliance monitoring, and annual planning cycles across CTE departments, special revenue, procurement, and campus staff. The SOP defines clear roles and approval workflows, a yearly purchasing calendar aligned with federal deadlines, and mandatory documentation requirements including Title I Crate uploads, inventory tracking, and compliance certifications. It is designed to ensure audit-ready documentation, equitable program improvement, and timely project completion while maintaining separation of responsibilities between CTE and Special Revenue departments.

### `Dallas ISD 2026-2027 Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment.pdf`

- **tags:** `perkins-v-clna`, `dallas-isd-cte`, `2026-2027-needs-assessment`, `curriculum-administration`, `labor-market-alignment`, `career-institutes-expansion`, `special-population-analysis`, `performance-gaps`, `programs-of-study`, `cte-completer-tracking`, `ccmr-indicators`, `equity-and-access`
- **entities:** `Dallas Independent School District`, `Perkins V`, `TEAL`, `CTER`, `WDA 6 Greater Dallas`, `Career Institute North`, `Career Institute South`, `Career Institute East`, `Townview School of Health`, `Townview School of Ed`, `Carter High School`, `Lincoln High School`
- **artifacts:** `comprehensive-local-needs-assessment`, `draft-document`, `performance-report`, `labor-market-analysis`, `program-evaluation`, `data-summary`
- **summary:** This draft CLNA for Dallas ISD's Perkins V grant maps student performance data, labor market alignment, and program gaps across 51 CTE programs in 13 career clusters. It identifies that the district missed several Perkins indicators—particularly 4-year graduation rate, reading/ELA proficiency, post-program placement, and postsecondary credential attainment—while highlighting that CTE concentrators generally outperform non-CTE peers except in postsecondary credentialing. The document details a major demographic shift with English Learners rising from 40.6% to 58.3% of the CTE population, analyzes gender and race gaps, and outlines strategies for special populations. It also documents the district's expansion of Career Institutes to close alignment gaps in Health Science and Architecture/Construction, projecting ~9,560 CI enrollees over four years, and sets a 35% IBC+completer goal against a current 13.2% rate for the Class of 2026.

- **summary:** This draft 2026-2027 CLNA for Dallas ISD Perkins V maps student performance gaps, special population shifts, and gender/race disparities across five high-demand career clusters. It documents that CTE learners outperform non-CTE peers on most indicators but underperform on postsecondary credential attainment (5S1), with the IBC+completer gap (13.2% vs. 35% goal) as the central CCMR challenge. The document details a demographic shift—EB/EL students rose from 40.6% to 58.3%—and outlines strategies including Career Institute expansion to close enrollment gaps in Health Science and Architecture/Construction, bilingual CTE support, and targeted recruitment for underrepresented groups. It serves as a coordinator-review draft requiring TEAL/CTER data insertion before final submission.

### `Documents/ACTE Vision 2025/Admin 201/Improving_CTE_Performance_Measurement_Data_Collection_09-11-2024.pdf`

- **tags:** `perkins-v-state-plan-guide`, `perkins-v-consolidated-annual-report`, `federal-register-notice`, `performance-measurement-data-collection`, `cte-concentrator-cohort-definition`, `active-cohort-vs-exit-cohort`, `elementary-secondary-act-alignment`, `core-indicators-numerator-denominator-specifications`, `state-determined-performance-levels`, `public-comment-invitation-september-2024`, `car-oct-2024-federal-guidance`, `cte-research-network-synthesis`, `work-based-learning-scaling-challenges`, `equity-access-gaps-cte-programs`, `cte-teacher-shortages`, `federal-education-policy-2024`
- **entities:** `U.S. Department of Education Office of Career Technical and Adult Education`, `Carl D. Perkins Career and Technical Education Act of 2006 (Perkins V)`, `Federal Register 89 FR 73638 (September 11 2024)`, `Federal Register 89 FR 73639 (September 11 2024)`, `What Works Clearinghouse standards`, `CTE Research Network synthesis 2024`, `Elementary and Secondary Education Act (ESEA) Title I Part A`
- **artifacts:** `federal-register-notice-pdf`, `policy-guidance-document`, `data-collection-specification`, `performance-measurement-framework`, `comment-request-documentation`, `state-plan-guide-reference`, `consolidated-annual-report-template-clarification`
- **summary:** This September 2024 U.S. Department of Education Federal Register notice invites public comment on proposed revisions to Perkins V's State Plan Guide and Consolidated Annual Report, aiming to standardize CTE performance measurement and data collection across states. The document addresses inconsistencies in how states define CTE concentrators—specifically the tension between 'active' cohorts tracking students across multiple grades versus 'exit' cohorts reporting proficiency after program completion—proposing a shift toward exit-cohort reporting aligned with ESEA assessment practices. The guidance seeks to fill data specification gaps for core indicator numerators/denominators, improve cross-state consistency, and ensure federal reporting aligns with statutory requirements, ultimately providing better information to improve CTE programs for student success and employer needs.

- **summary:** This PDF serves as a quick-reference guide for CTE administrators, detailing the six federal Perkins V performance measures—graduation rates, academic proficiency, postsecondary placement, gender equity, program quality, and employment in field of study. It defines each measure, explains how data is collected (e.g., ACGR, ESSA standards), and clarifies the purpose behind tracking student outcomes. The document is essential for ensuring federal compliance, guiding program improvement, and aligning CTE pathways with workforce and academic goals.

- **artifacts:** `presentation-deck`, `research-survey-report`, `messaging-guidance`, `strategic-planning-doc`
- **summary:** This December 2025 presentation deck by Advance CTE Communications Associate Stacy Whitehouse shares findings from the national CTE perceptions survey (2023-2025), tracking awareness, favorability, funding support, and generational differences—particularly Gen Z skepticism. It emphasizes that while CTE awareness and bipartisan funding support are growing, misconceptions persist about who CTE serves, and highlights key motivations (financial independence, hands-on learning) and priority elements for parents. The deck serves as a strategic resource for state and district leaders to refine recruitment messaging, align CTE programs with family priorities, and advocate for expanded CTE funding and pathways.

- **summary:** This document presents MCVSD's Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) as a strategic flight plan for continuous improvement in CTE, moving beyond compliance to a collaborative process driven by stakeholder engagement and data analysis. It outlines a 24-month implementation plan with tiered stakeholder strategies, tools like the ACT framework, SWOT analysis, and RACI matrices to address resource constraints, workforce alignment, and data collection. The plan culminates in district alignment activities beginning in January 2026, including Perkins integration and metric setting, supported by AI-assisted resource generation.

### `Documents/New Stipend Request Form[29].pdf`

- **tags:** `stipend-request-form`, `cte-transportation-compensation`, `specialized-driver-pay`, `multi-level-approval-workflow`, `central-go-budget-funding`, `hazardous-material-transport-stipend`, `mid-day-shuttle-stipend`, `curriculum-and-instruction-administration`, `hr-compensation-form`, `dallas-isd-cte-stipend`
- **entities:** `Human Capital Management`, `Central GO Budget`, `Oracle job codes (6010`, `6020`, `6030`, `6040)`, `HR-C2103`
- **artifacts:** `stipend-request-form`, `hr-compensation-document`, `approval-workflow`, `budget-justification-form`
- **summary:** This is the Dallas ISD Human Capital Management stipend request form (HR-C2103) designed to create or modify specialized stipends for CTE transportation staff. It captures justification for paying drivers who manage complex mid-day shuttles, multi-campus navigation, and the transport of hazardous materials or heavy trailers. The form requires a multi-step approval chain—from department director through executive director, area chief, budget approval, and potentially the board of trustees if the budget impact exceeds $50,000. The request typically ranges from $30,000 to $50,000 and aims to retain skilled personnel for CTE programs by aligning pay with private-sector demands.

It outlines a multi-step approval workflow—from department director through executive leadership and the Board of Trustees—with specific thresholds triggering additional review (e.g., budget impacts over $50,000). The form is used to request specialized stipends for CTE drivers managing after-hours shuttles and multi-campus navigation, funded via the Central GO Budget using line code 199-11-6118-ED-921-X-22, with an estimated cost range of $30,000–$50,000 based on a $50-per-trip cap of ten trips per staff member annually.

### `NICHQ Teacher.pdf`

- **tags:** `vanderbilt-assessment-teacher`, `nichq-behavioral-rating-scale`, `adhd-screening-tool`, `classroom-performance-tracker`, `teacher-informant-form`, `student-behavior-evaluation`, `academic-performance-rubric`, `pediatric-behavioral-health`, `assessment-compliance-doc`, `special-education-referral`

- **artifacts:** `assessment-form`, `rating-scale`, `teacher-observation-tool`, `tracking-document`, `compliance-form`
- **summary:** This is the NICHQ Vanderbilt Assessment Scale—Teacher Informant, a standardized tool used by educators to rate student behaviors and academic performance since the start of the school year. It supports ADHD screening, classroom performance tracking, and special education referrals by collecting symptom ratings and performance scores across academics and classroom behavior. The form includes sections for symptom frequency, academic grades, and classroom conduct, with totals calculated for office review. It is a widely recognized pediatric behavioral health document adapted from Dr. Mark L. Wolraich’s rating scales.

- **summary:** This 2026-27 Texas Perkins V Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) Guidebook provides the structured framework for local CTE leaders to conduct a full program evaluation every two years. It walks stakeholders through seven core sections—from student performance and labor market alignment to equity and educator recruitment—using disaggregated data from the CTER/TEAL systems. The guidebook ensures that CLNA findings directly drive the local Perkins application and spending decisions, emphasizing continuous improvement and equitable access for special populations.

### `Perkins Travel/Dallas Independent School District - Plan without Addendums (37)[39].pdf`

- **tags:** `district-improvement-plan-2025-2026`, `curriculum-administration`, `racial-equity-initiative`, `student-achievement-goals`, `title-i-programming`, `p-t-ech-echs-expansion`, `ap-expansion`, `dual-language-programs`, `comprehensive-needs-assessment`, `budget-modernization-2015-present`, `strategic-pillars-implementation`, `goal-setting-2030-targets`

- **artifacts:** `district-improvement-plan`, `comprehensive-needs-assessment`, `strategic-planning-document`, `title-i-descriptor-document`, `goal-mapping-report`
- **summary:** This 71-page Dallas ISD District Improvement Plan for 2025-2026 establishes five student outcome goals and four resource constraints guiding campus and district improvement work. It frames academic targets around third-grade reading/mathematics and eighth-grade reading/mathematics proficiency, plus a college, career, and military readiness (CCMR) goal extending to 2030. The plan is built on a comprehensive needs assessment documenting demographics, student learning gaps, program strengths, and stakeholder perceptions. It weaves racial equity into six strategic pillars, details Title I program descriptors ranging from student progress monitoring to parent engagement, and tracks $2+ billion in modernization investments since 2015. The document serves as both a accountability roadmap and a working document for strategic adjustments based on data and stakeholder feedback.

- **summary:** This document defines the Texas CTE student classification system used for state accountability reporting, mapping students into categories ranging from Non-CTE (Code 4) through CTE Completers (Code 7) based on course enrollment, credit accumulation, and program of study completion. It explains special cases like CTE Explorers (Code E), who meet concentrator or completer criteria but are in unapproved regional programs, and provides the regulatory basis under 19 TAC Chapters 126, 127, and 130. The coding is critical for district reporting, state funding, and equity tracking of CTE pathway completion across ESC regions.
